The work still grows with the size of the edit, and a very large edit is still bounded only by the existing per-edit limits described in `docs/api/docs/validation.md` § Delta content; this change removes wasted memory and repeated work, not that growth. **Second, duplicate sibling names now resolve deterministically.** Two siblings can legitimately end up sharing a name, because renaming a prim under its current parent is unconditional and does not check for a collision — that is the long-standing contract and it is unchanged. When a later edit then collides with that name, the service has to choose which of the sharers the collision applies to; previously that choice came out of an in-memory hash ordering, so two replicas — or the same history replayed during a disaster-recovery rebuild — could choose differently and delete *different* subtrees. The choice is now always the sharer with the **lowest section id**, which fixes which subtree is removed when a new prim takes a taken name, which side of a move-with-conflict is removed, and which id is named in the `INVALID_ARGUMENT` rejection a colliding rename returns. **This narrows which sibling an edit can destroy; it does not widen who can destroy one** — any client already permitted to write the layer could always do this with a legal edit, subject to that same permission. **Third, replacing a layer's whole contents in place now clears the ordering of its top-level prims.** That ordering was previously left behind, and the next edit read it back as phantom children, re-persisting it indefinitely. This changes stored state only — no client sees a different response, and no wire format changed. **Related internal change, invisible from outside:** creating a layer, rebuilding one from a backup, and replaying backups no longer assemble a list of storage changes that is immediately discarded. **No configuration change, no new environment variable, no new or changed metric, no changed default, and no wire or API change.** See `docs/api/docs/validation.md` § Delta content for the sibling-name-collision rules.
- **`UpdateLayer` hot-path correctness: CreateLayer-overwrite race, Lua f64 set_order overflow, MAXLEN precision, slow-path partial-header guard, fast/slow-path numeric-formatting parity, and a redundant cancel-classification arm** (bug `b.qw6`). Six bundled correctness fixes on the `UpdateLayer` hot path that together close the remaining "in-flight `UpdateLayer` commits to a doomed object" / "Lua silently corrupts past 2^53" / "fast-path vs slow-path divergence past ~10^14" surfaces. (1) *CreateLayer-overwrite vs `UpdateLayer` race (High).* Pre-b.qw6, `CreateLayer` overwrite flipped the `(session_id, layer_name) → object_id` mapping ahead of an async UNLINK of the old object's keys (30-second grace window). An `UpdateLayer` that resolved the layer mapping just before the flip would still commit to the old `object_id`; its subscribers — already re-bootstrapped onto the new stream — would never see the update. The overwrite path now follows a strict four-step order: (a) write the new object's header, (b) `HSET le:{old_oid}:obj:hdr superseded 1` on the OLD object's header (single command, single Cluster slot), (c) flip the mapping, (d) schedule async cleanup. The `UpdateLayer` slow path adds `superseded` to its WATCHed `HGETALL` reply and aborts with `FAILED_PRECONDITION("object superseded by a concurrent CreateLayer overwrite")` if set; the Lua fast path issues `HGET le:{oid}:obj:hdr superseded` immediately after the partial-header guard and returns `SUPERSEDED:` error reply with the same wire message. Co-exists with b.j9j's independent `ext_gc_scheduled` flag on the same header (different field, different writer, no collision). (2) *Lua `set_order` f64 overflow (Critical).* The fast path's `next_set_order + delta_set_order` arithmetic silently rounded past 2^53 because Lua 5.1 `number` is IEEE 754 f64 — two distinct `SetField`s could land on the same stored `set_order` and diff reconstruction's per-name ordering would break. Pre-fix the slow path already returned `DeltaError::SetOrderOverflow` from its `checked_add` arm, but routed it to gRPC `INVALID_ARGUMENT` with a `u64::MAX`-based message that the runtime could never reach (the Lua ceiling tripped first when crossed via the fast path). Both paths now reject at the same 2^53 − 1 ceiling: fast path returns `SET_ORDER_OVERFLOW` error reply, slow path returns the same `DeltaError::SetOrderOverflow`, and both now route through `From<DeltaError> for ServiceError` to gRPC `INTERNAL` (non-retriable). The `INVALID_ARGUMENT` → `INTERNAL` change on the slow path is the user-visible part; it matches the existing `SequenceOverflow` posture, points operators at the real root cause (a long-lived object that accumulated 2^53 set_order advancements), and removes the misleading "your delta is invalid" framing. Upstream, `validate_delta_bounds` in `src/domain/validation.rs` rejects any `SetField` whose `delta.set_order` exceeds 2^53 − 1 with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`DeltaBoundsExceeded`) so a single client-shaped delta cannot push past the ceiling in one step — that case remains a client-attributable error. (3) *Lua MAXLEN tonumber precision (High).* `LIVE_EDIT_LAYER_STREAM_MAXLEN` is now range-clamped at startup to ≤ 2^53 − 1; values above the ceiling fail fast in `Config::from_env`. Pre-fix a misconfigured very-large MAXLEN could lose precision on the Lua side and produce non-deterministic stream trimming. (4) *Slow-path partial-header guard parity (Medium).* The Lua fast path's `seq`-missing check (b.k1r) is now mirrored on the slow path. Pre-fix a header that existed but had no `seq` field (corrupted partial write, crash mid-overwrite, hand-written test fixture) caused `header_to_object_state` to compute `state.sequence_num = 0` on the slow path and the commit attempted `new_seq = 0 + 1 = 1`, colliding with the initial `1-0` stream entry from `CreateLayer`. The slow path now checks `!header.contains_key("seq")` after the empty-header check and returns `FailedPrecondition("object header partial (seq missing or malformed)")`, UNWATCHing synchronously. (5) *Fast/slow-path numeric-formatting parity past ~10^14 (Medium, surfaced by the b.qw6 parity proptest).* The Lua script previously formatted integer values via `tostring(N)`, which Lua 5.1 routes through `%.14g`-style formatting and loses integer precision around ~10^14 even though the underlying f64 represents integers up to 2^53. The slow path stores integer-formatted strings everywhere, so the script's `tostring` formatter produced byte-identical state only up to ~10^14 and diverged past that — a fast-path/slow-path parity bug that the existing parity proptest would have surfaced once it exercised high `set_order` cursors. The script now formats every numeric value through `string.format("%d", N)` so both paths produce byte-identical formatting at every cursor value up to the 2^53 − 1 ceiling. **Operator impact for older data**: existing layers whose `set_order` or `next_set_order` happened to land above ~10^14 may show formatting differences between data written pre-b.qw6 and post-b.qw6; the values themselves are equal, only the stored string representation differs. Diff reconstruction handles both representations correctly via `tonumber`-on-read. No data migration required; no client-visible behavior change. (6) *Redundant cancel-classification arm dropped (Medium).* The slow path's EXEC reply previously had a dedicated `is_canceled()` arm alongside the unified `map_transient_fred_err` classifier; `is_transient_fred_error` already classifies `Canceled`, so the special-case arm was dead code. Removed for clarity — no behavior change. **Client impact:** `UpdateLayer` callers seeing `FAILED_PRECONDITION` should be prepared for the new "object superseded by a concurrent CreateLayer overwrite" message alongside the established stale-`object_id` mismatch case; both indicate the object the client was holding is no longer the live one and the client should re-resolve the mapping. That same message now also reaches a caller whose write races the background clean-up of a layer whose mapping is already gone — the re-resolve then reports the layer as not found, and the client action is unchanged. `UpdateLayer` callers seeing `SetOrderOverflow` previously received `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; they now receive `INTERNAL`. The trigger is the object's accumulated history rather than the client's delta, so no retry can succeed — operators should overwrite the layer via `CreateLayer` (new `object_id` generation) to escape the ceiling. The Lua precision bug (item 5) is invisible to clients (state continues to be correct on both backends and across the boundary), but operators investigating layers with `set_order` past ~10^14 will see the stored string representation change at the b.qw6 upgrade boundary. No wire-format change.
- **External-value GC ref_count integrity hardening: partial-failure double-schedule guard, drain race during replica boot, per-hash transient-error tolerance, and an underflow visibility counter** (bug `b.j9j`). Four bundled correctness fixes on the external-value GC pipeline that together close the remaining "blob still alive but ref_count wrong" surface left over after `b.kf1`'s C-5 atomic decrement+delete script. Each previously corrupted the `ext_meta` ref_count silently — no metric, no log, no client-observable signal until enough corruption accumulated that the next GC sweep DEL'd a blob another live layer was still pointing at. (1) *`schedule_old_object_ext_gc` partial-failure re-schedule.* `delete_layer`'s cleanup runs in two phases — step 4 schedules external-value GC for the old object's hashes, step 5 UNLINKs the old object's index keys. Pre-b.j9j, a crash or transient Valkey error between step 4 and step 5 left the `obj:hdr` alive without any signal that GC had already been scheduled; the next periodic orphan sweep (§5.5 step 5) would re-enter `schedule_old_object_ext_gc` on the same `old_oid`, add the same external-value hashes to `le:ext:{gc}:pending` a second time, and the GC worker would then double-decrement every blob — eventually DELing blobs that other live layers still pointed at. The fix writes `ext_gc_scheduled=1` on the `obj:hdr` after a successful `schedule_release` (gated on `EXISTS obj:hdr` so a step-5 race that won doesn't resurrect the header with a single-field shape); subsequent invocations read the flag, short-circuit with a debug log, and converge to a no-op. (2) *Startup drain double-decrement race.* `le:ext:{gc}:inflight` was a plain `SET` pre-b.j9j; a replica booting while another replica was mid-`process_pending` for hash H observed H in `inflight`, unconditionally `SADD`'d it back to `pending`, and the next leader sweep `SMOVE`d H to `inflight` a second time — decrementing the same `ref_count` twice. The fix migrates `inflight` to a sorted set keyed by claim timestamp (millisecond Unix epoch), introduces `gc_claim_pending.lua` to atomically `SREM` from `pending` + `ZADD` to `inflight` with the claim time (replacing the pre-b.j9j plain `SMOVE`, which would have lost the timestamp the drain now needs), and rewrites `drain_inflight_to_pending` to use `ZRANGEBYSCORE 0 cutoff_ms` where `cutoff_ms = now_ms − LEADER_LOCK_TTL_MS` (30 s). Only claims older than the leader-lock TTL are re-queued — a fresh claim must belong to a still-running sweep on another replica, because a crash-victim claim would have caused that replica's `le:gc:leader` lock to expire by now. The trailing release in step 3 of the per-hash protocol is now `ZREM inflight` (was `SREM inflight`) to match. (3) *Per-hash transient errors no longer abort the whole GC batch.* `process_pending` classifies fred errors via the shared `map_transient_fred_err` and tests the result with the new `is_transient_aborted` predicate (`ServiceError::Aborted("transient I/O: ...")`). A transient blip on a single hash's `gc_claim_pending` EVALSHA, `gc_decrement_and_collect` EVALSHA, or trailing `ZREM` now logs a `WARNING` and `continue`s to the next candidate in the 100-hash batch; the affected hash stays pinned in `pending` or `inflight` for the next sweep tick or startup drain to retry under the script's retry-idempotency contract (§11.3). Non-transient classifications still escalate out of the loop. Pre-b.j9j the first transient blip on any hash in a 100-hash batch aborted the entire batch and lengthened the time-to-recover for the rest of the pending set under a flapping Valkey. The orphan-sweep half (`cleanup_orphaned_object` / `sweep_orphaned_layers` in `src/storage/orphan_sweep.rs`) was tightened the same way — `SweepError` is now propagated on every Valkey result that pre-b.j9j fell through `unwrap_or_default()` (the per-orphan `SMEMBERS` / `ZRANGE` index walks, the `EXISTS session meta` orphaned-mapping check, and the per-mapping `SCARD layers index` cleanup), so a partial-cleanup state can no longer strand on a Valkey blip — the next sweep interval observes the residual keys and retries cleanly under the new `ext_gc_scheduled` guard. (4) *Underflow visibility counter.* `gc_decrement_and_collect.lua` now returns a distinct `"deleted_underflow"` action when the post-decrement `ref_count` goes below zero — the double-decrement / corruption signal. The data-plane outcome is identical to `"deleted"` (both still DEL meta + data), but the underflow path additionally emits a Lua-side `redis.log(redis.LOG_WARNING, ...)` carrying the hash hex and bumps the new `live_edit_gc_underflow_total` Prometheus counter so operator dashboards can alert on sustained underflow. A small non-zero baseline is benign (the C-5 retry-idempotency contract deliberately produces `("deleted_underflow", -1)` on a wire-loss replay where the retried EVAL lands on an already-deleted meta — see SDD §11.3); a sustained rate against a healthy fleet is the actionable signal that two GC paths are concurrently decrementing the same hash with only one outstanding release scheduled. The counter is wired into production via `ValkeyExternalValueStore::with_metrics()` (builder-style attach so unit tests can construct the store without a metrics registry while `main.rs` always attaches it); the same metrics handle is also attached to the orphan-sweep wrapper so both call sites bump the counter symmetrically. **Client impact:** none — all four items are server-internal correctness fixes. Subscribers continue to receive `INTERNAL` from `SubscribeReadLayer` when an `ext_data` blob is missing (the b.kf1 H-stor-10 contract is unchanged); the b.j9j fixes prevent the *root cause* of that surface error from reaching it. No wire-format change.
- **Domain section-tree invariant hardening: write-path depth cap, `DiffChangeSection` rename collision detection, `CommandReorderChildren` duplicate-id rejection, `is_descendant_of` self-parent escape, and `apply_delta` partial-mutation contract** (bug `b.dmu`). Six bundled correctness fixes on the delta-application engine that together close the remaining "unrecoverable stuck layer" surface left over after `b.55p`'s read-path depth cap. Each previously silently corrupted state or produced an unrecoverable layer; the new behaviour is `INVALID_ARGUMENT` at delta-apply time. (1) *Write-path `MAX_SECTION_TREE_DEPTH` enforcement.* The 10,000-section depth cap is now checked on every `apply_delta` after structural commands have applied but before the `sequence_num` commit; a breach returns `DeltaError::SectionTreeTooDeep { depth, cap }` → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Pre-fix the cap lived only on the read path (`DiffEngine::sections_in_dfs_order`), so an attacker could accumulate depth across many small deltas (each individually under `MAX_CHILDREN_LIST_LEN` and `MAX_STRUCT_COMMANDS`) and leave the layer permanently stuck — `cascade_delete_section` walks `state.children` with no depth bound and would itself crash on the cleanup cascade, so the read-side `INTERNAL` was the only client-observable signal and there was no `UpdateLayer`-shaped recovery. Post-fix any object whose history flows exclusively through the post-b.dmu engine satisfies the depth invariant by construction; the read-side `INTERNAL` is reserved for legacy stored state predating the fix. Clients seeing it on a legacy layer should overwrite via `CreateLayer` (new object generation) rather than `UpdateLayer`. (2) *`DiffChangeSection` rename onto an existing sibling's `(parent_id, section_name)` slot now rejects with the new `DeltaError::DuplicateSiblingName { section_id, parent_id, conflicting_id, name }` → `INVALID_ARGUMENT` on the existing-section (rename) branch.* Mirrors `MoveSection` edges 4 & 5. Pre-fix the rename branch stored both siblings with the same `(parent_id, name)`; diff reconstruction's per-name child lookup then returned an arbitrary one of the two, observable downstream only as missing or mis-routed sections on subscribers. The *new-section* branch keeps the legacy cascade-delete-the-conflicting-sibling behaviour from `CreateSection`'s PRD §2.3.4 policy — that branch carries no "client wanted to disambiguate" signal because the conflict is between a fresh id and an old one. (3) *`CommandReorderChildren` whose `children_list` repeats a `section_id` now rejects with the new `DeltaError::DuplicateInReorder { parent_id, section_id }` → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`.* Pre-fix the per-id existence + parent-match check accepted `[1, 2, 1, 3]` because every id satisfied both predicates; the order was then stored verbatim and the read-path DFS visited the same section twice with its position information permanently scrambled. (4) *`is_descendant_of` self-parent silent escape closed.* The prior implementation guarded the parent-walk arm with `sec.parent_id != candidate` and fell through to `return false` on match, silently reporting "not a descendant" for a self-parenting section and letting `apply_move_section`'s cycle check pass for a target that was its own parent. The guard is removed; the cycle is now caught by the visited-set check that already covered general cycles in stored state. (5) *`apply_delta` deferred `sequence_num` commit to end-of-function* — pre-fix the bump ran before any mutation, so a delta where command N is invalid but commands 1..N-1 are valid still advanced `sequence_num` past the point where its partial mutations could be safely reloaded. The bump now runs only after every helper has succeeded; a mid-delta error leaves `state.sequence_num` at its prior value. The other state maps may still be partially mutated — see (6). (6) *`apply_delta` partial-mutation contract is now documented in rustdoc.* On `Err(_)` callers MUST discard the partially-mutated `state` and reload a fresh snapshot before retrying; only `state.sequence_num` is guaranteed unchanged. `LayerStore::update_layer` and `LayerStore::create_layer` already honour this — `state` is rebuilt from Valkey on every attempt — but the contract was previously implicit. **Client impact:** clients producing well-formed deltas (no duplicate sibling-names, no repeated reorder ids, depth within 10,000) are unaffected. A previously-accepted delta hitting any of the three new write-path rules now fails fast with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` at the gRPC handler before any state mutation lands. The clearest operator-side audit is dashboards on `live_edit_delta_create_section_skipped_missing_parent_total` (b.39x) for evidence of producer bugs that emit malformed deltas, then traffic graphs on `INVALID_ARGUMENT` rate for the `UpdateLayer` endpoint after rollout. No wire-format change; the three new `DeltaError` variants map to existing gRPC status codes.
- **Helm chart `service.grpc.port` and the gRPC liveness / readiness probe ports realigned to `50001`, matching the binary default and the Dockerfile `EXPOSE` directive** (bug `b.bm8`). Pre-b.bm8 `helm/live-edit-service/values.yaml` declared `service.grpc.port: 50051`, `livenessProbe.grpc.port: 50051`, and `readinessProbe.grpc.port: 50051`, while the binary has always defaulted `LIVE_EDIT_PORT` to `50001` and the Dockerfile has always declared `EXPOSE 50001 9090`. The mismatch only worked in deployments that already overrode `LIVE_EDIT_PORT=50051` via the env-block or values override; a chart install with only the chart defaults — the supported quick-start path — produced pods whose gRPC server listened on `50001` while the Service mapped traffic to `50051` and the kubelet probed `50051`, leaving every replica failing readiness in a `CrashLoopBackOff`-adjacent state with no traffic ever routed. The three port values are now realigned to `50001` so the chart defaults install cleanly. Operators who legitimately want the service on `50051` (e.g. matching a pre-existing Service abstraction) must now set both `service.grpc.port: 50051` *and* `LIVE_EDIT_PORT=50051` on the pod env-block; pre-fix deployments that already pinned `LIVE_EDIT_PORT=50051` need no change. The values.yaml comment block on `service.grpc.port` now explicitly calls out the three-way invariant (binary default ↔ Dockerfile `EXPOSE` ↔ chart `service.grpc.port`) so a future operator change drifts no part of the trio without touching the others. No wire-format change.
- **Docker builder-stage smoke test now asserts on the binary's exit code instead of grepping stderr for a specific message** (bug `b.bm8`). Pre-b.bm8 the smoke test ran `env -i /build/target/release/live-edit 2>&1 | grep -q "configuration error"` to prove the binary loads and runs past static initialization. A `tracing` reformatter (e.g. the JSON-default change for `LIVE_EDIT_LOG_FORMAT`) or any future refactor of `Config::from_env`'s panic message would silently flip the grep from match-on-known-bad to non-match-on-known-bad, bypassing the smoke test entirely while reporting build success. The check now invokes the binary with `env -i`, captures `$?`, and asserts the exit code is `1` (the canonical "fail-fast on missing required env var" exit code that `main()` produces). A binary that segfaults on bad libstdc++ symbols or aborts in static init returns a different code and fails the build at the smoke-test step, where the Dockerfile already documents that the failure is meaningful. No runtime behavior change.
- **Docker builder-stage `ldd` check now propagates failure of `ldd` itself, not only the grep result** (bug `b.bm8`). The pre-b.bm8 invocation was `RUN ldd /build/target/release/live-edit | grep -q libstdc++ && (echo BUILD FAIL ... && exit 1) || true`; under that shape any failure of `ldd` (binary missing, ELF unreadable, glibc loader error) silently fell through the trailing `|| true` and the build proceeded with the unverified binary. The check now uses an `if ldd ... | grep -q libstdc++; then ... exit 1; fi` shape so the default `set -e` propagates a failing `ldd` exit status. Symmetry with the smoke-test exit-code change above: both checks now hard-fail on any unexpected outcome rather than silently degrading to "skip."
- **Auth singleflight no longer issues duplicate backend calls in the cache-insert / inflight-remove gap, and a backend panic no longer cascades to every coalesced waiter** (bug `b.ohw` H-auth-2 + H-auth-3). Two correctness bugs in the per-key singleflight that coalesces concurrent auth lookups (`AuthCache`). (1) The post-resolution sequence was `inflight.remove(key) → cache.insert(key, result)`; a fresh caller arriving in the gap after remove but before insert observed an empty cache *and* an empty inflight map and issued a duplicate backend call. The order is now `cache.insert → inflight.remove`, so a caller scheduled in any window either reads the cached value (already inserted) or joins the still-live shared future (inflight not yet removed). (2) A panic inside the backend client implementation (`PermissionsClient::check_permission` or `UserinfoClient::get_current_user`) propagated through `futures::Shared` to every coalesced waiter as a panic each — N concurrent callers all crashed instead of one. The inner future is now wrapped in `AssertUnwindSafe(...).catch_unwind()`; caught panics surface as a clean `AuthError::InvalidResponse("internal panic in auth backend")` to one caller and as the same error to other waiters, never as a panic. Both fixes are internal — no wire-format change, no operator action required.
- **External-value lifecycle hardening: subscribers see `INTERNAL` instead of an empty blob on missing external value, plus internal correctness fixes** (bug `b.kf1`). Six bundled fixes on the external-value GC pipeline and the subscribe-path read of an external blob. (1) *`SubscribeReadLayer` no longer silently returns an empty blob when an external value is missing.* Pre-b.kf1, `build_diff_payload` substituted `Vec::new()` for any external blob absent from the resolved batch — silent corruption that the client could not detect. The subscribe path now hard-errors with `ServiceError::Internal("external value missing: {hash_hex}")` → gRPC `INTERNAL` (b.kf1 H-stor-10). Subscribers should treat `INTERNAL` from `SubscribeReadLayer` as "this replica observed an inconsistent state" and retry against a healthy replica. (2) *External-value GC decrement+delete is now atomic.* The `HINCRBY ext_meta ref_count -1` and the conditional `MULTI { DEL meta; DEL data } EXEC` were previously two unguarded round-trips. A process death between them left ref_count decremented but the keys alive; the startup drain re-queued the hash and a retry double-decremented, eventually deleting blobs another live layer still pointed at. The two round-trips collapse into a single atomic `EVALSHA gc_decrement_and_collect.lua` (b.kf1 C-5) that is retry-idempotent — HINCRBY on a missing meta key auto-creates it with `ref_count = -1` which the script's `<= 0` branch treats as DELETE on already-absent keys (no-op). (3) *Orphan sweep now schedules external-value GC.* Pre-b.kf1, the orphan sweep UNLINKed per-field hashes for orphaned objects but did NOT touch `ext_meta`/`ext_data`, so every external value referenced by a crash-orphaned object had its ref_count permanently inflated and the blob could never be GC'd — a silent disk-leak path proportional to crash frequency × external-value churn. `cleanup_orphaned_object` now calls `schedule_old_object_ext_gc` BEFORE issuing the per-object UNLINKs (b.kf1 H-stor-1). (4) *Orphan sweep is now periodic.* Pre-b.kf1 it ran only at startup, so a long-lived replica accumulated orphans for the duration of its uptime. The sweep now re-runs every 300 s (`ORPHAN_SWEEP_INTERVAL`); each tick is gated behind the shared `le:gc:leader` lock so only one replica per fleet sweeps at a time. (5) *GC + orphan sweeps are leader-gated to one replica.* Pre-b.kf1, every replica in an N-replica fleet ran the GC sweep on every tick, multiplying GC load by N and risking interleaved SMOVEs on overlapping pending entries. Both `spawn_gc_task` and `spawn_orphan_sweep` now claim a short-TTL `SET le:gc:leader <replica_id> EX 30 NX` lock at the start of each tick; non-leaders skip the tick. The lock is documented as a *load-shed optimization, not a correctness primitive* — per-hash atomicity is guaranteed by `SMOVE pending → inflight` + the C-5 Lua script regardless of how many replicas sweep. (6) *Maintenance pool isolation.* All GC and orphan-sweep Valkey traffic flows through the new dedicated `maintenance_pool` (sized via `LIVE_EDIT_MAINTENANCE_POOL_SIZE`, default 1) rather than the multiplexed data pool, so writers/readers cannot queue behind a long SCAN page or HGETALL pass and conversely a saturated data pool cannot stall sweeps (b.kf1 H-stor-5). **Client impact:** subscribers receive `INTERNAL` (instead of an empty blob) if an external value is missing during diff reconstruction — treat as retriable against a healthy replica. The remaining items are internal correctness fixes with no user-facing API change. No wire-format change.
- **`UpdateLayer` commit-path hardening: WATCH shutdown drain, EXEC transient-I/O parity, overwrite-cleanup UNLINK chunking, diff-read regime fix, and Lua / slow-path sequence-number ceiling** (bug `b.k1r`). Five bundled fixes on the `update_layer` commit path plus shared `cleanup_old_object` / orphan-sweep cleanup. (1) *WatchGuard Drop spawns were untracked.* Every `update_layer` slow-path early-return that had issued a WATCH spawned a fire-and-forget `tokio::spawn` to UNWATCH the txn-pool client; on shutdown the spawn could race the txn-pool `quit()` and leave stale WATCH state on the just-closed connection. All such spawns are now registered on a shared `tokio_util::task::TaskTracker` that `ValkeyClient::quit` drains (with a 2-second cap matching the per-pool quit budget) before closing any pool, so shutdown cannot tear the runtime down mid-`unwatch().await`. An inflight count is exposed on the new `live_edit_storage_watch_unwatch_inflight` gauge so operators can see outstanding UNWATCHes at scrape time. (2) *EXEC-reply retry classification asymmetry* — see the dedicated Changed entry above. (3) *`cleanup_old_object` (CreateLayer overwrite path) and the orphan sweep issued a single UNLINK over the entire to-retire key list* — on layers with tens of thousands of keys this could overshoot Valkey's `proto-max-bulk-len` and surface as "Protocol error" or a hard disconnect. Both sites now chunk at 500 keys per UNLINK; all keys share the retired object's `{object_id}` hash tag so the chunks remain slot-safe. (4) *`load_object_snapshot` corrupted its own `object_id` on partial-header reads.* A tortured expression `parse_u64(&header, "seq").map(|_| object_id.parse::<u64>().unwrap_or(0)).unwrap_or(0)` discarded the parsed `seq` with `|_|` and degraded the caller-supplied `object_id` to `0` whenever the header happened to be missing `seq`, propagating that `0` through every downstream log line and through the diff-regime dispatch. Fixed by parsing the caller-supplied `object_id` unconditionally (it's the scope of the entire load) and switching the other u64 fields to a `parse_u64_warn` helper that logs at `warn!` on corrupt-but-present headers instead of silently defaulting to 0. (5) *Lua fast path's `current_seq + 1` silently stalled past 2^53.* Lua 5.1 `number` is IEEE 754 f64, so integer precision drops at 2^53 and the script would keep reusing the same `new_seq`. Both backends now reject at `current_seq >= 2^53 - 1`: the fast path returns `SEQ_OVERFLOW` in its error reply (surfaced as `INTERNAL`, non-retriable), the slow path returns `DeltaError::SequenceOverflow` at the same ceiling. Matching at the minimum of what both backends can safely represent means the `LIVE_EDIT_UPDATE_LAYER_BACKEND=txn` rollback knob can't resurrect an object the fast path would reject. Alongside: a defensive partial-header guard in the Lua script rejects headers where `seq` is absent or non-numeric as `NOT_FOUND` (previously fell through to `new_seq = 1` and collided with the initial `1-0` stream entry); `validate_client_id` now rejects `|` (see Changed); a `now_micros` helper consolidated from `storage::session`, `storage::layer`, and `grpc::ping` into `domain::time::now_micros` with saturating-to-zero on pre-epoch clocks (replaces three copies each of which would panic on clock rollback); `hex_decode` / `hex_decode_f64` emit warn logs on malformed input (preserves the empty-string / 0.0 fallback for infallible callers); `parse_u64` warn logs on corrupt header fields throughout `storage::layer` / `storage::diff_read`; `KeySchema::obj_field_from_member` / `obj_ts_from_member` helpers pull the key-shape concat off the call sites. **Operator impact:** expect fewer spurious `INTERNAL` during Valkey restarts (EXEC-site retry); the new gauge is populated on every `/metrics` scrape and a sustained non-zero value there indicates an UNWATCH failing to complete — investigate Valkey reachability / command timeouts. No wire or API changes; the gRPC contract on all three surface paths is unchanged.
- **gRPC boundary hardening bundle: transport-drop classification, streaming chunk-count DoS, client_id validation, presence writer-task hang on client drop, and unbounded subscriber consumer channel** (bug `b.jr8`). Five interlocking gRPC-layer bugs closed together. (1) *Transport drops on `CreateLayer` / `UpdateLayer` mis-classified.* Mid-stream TCP resets and client crashes surfaced as `INTERNAL` instead of `CANCELLED`; clients could not tell a server fault from their own peer hanging up. Now mapped via the new `ServiceError::Cancelled` → gRPC `CANCELLED` (see Changed). (2) *Chunk-count DoS on client-streaming RPCs.* The 100 MB byte cap did not bound per-chunk framing overhead — a well-formed 100 MB upload split into millions of 1-byte chunks was a cheap CPU/scheduler amplifier. New `LIVE_EDIT_MAX_LAYER_SIZE / 1024` chunk-count cap closes it (see Changed). (3) *`client_id` accepted arbitrary bytes including control characters.* Enabled log-injection and stream-payload-framing corruption; now validated at the gRPC handler (see Changed). (4) *Presence writer task hung until next broadcast event after client drop.* The `JoinSession` server→client task only exited on cancellation-token or broadcast-closed signals; a silent HTTP/2 close left it alive until the next peer event attempted to send, delaying `PeerLeft` publication and Valkey presence-key cleanup. The task now also exits on `tx.closed().await`, so the shared cancellation token fires within the same event-loop tick as the client going dark. No change to the observable `PeerLeft` / session cleanup contract — only the latency is shorter. (5) *Unbounded subscriber consumer channel.* The in-process mpsc between the subscriber's `XREAD BLOCK` task and the gRPC send task was unbounded, so a dead-but-not-yet-detected subscriber could hold a `subscriber_pool` slot and an XREAD-BLOCK connection open indefinitely while its queue grew. Now bounded at 4,096 entries with a 5-second watchdog (see Changed). **Client impact:** clients now see `CANCELLED` on transport drop (before: `INTERNAL`); clients using oversized or control-character-laden `client_id` or streams with >100,000 chunks now see `INVALID_ARGUMENT` on first offense; well-behaved clients see no behavior change. Defense-in-depth items shipped alongside: short-TTL (2 s) `session_id → base_path` cache in front of the hot-path `HGET le:{sid}:sess base_path` lookup, invalidated immediately on `le:session:deleted` pub/sub events so staleness is bounded by TTL *and* active invalidation; `handle_update_layer` per-commit log downgraded from `info!` to `debug!` (see note to ops: runbooks grepping for the "UpdateLayer completed" line at info-level will stop seeing it by default — raise log level or re-grep at debug).
- **Session lifecycle: `CreateSession` duplicate-name race, `DeleteSession` EXEC connection-drop mis-classification, and `DeleteSession` cascade amplification** (bug `b.91j`). Three session-layer correctness and reliability bugs fixed together. (1) *`CreateSession` duplicate-name race on Valkey Cluster.* The pre-fix duplicate-name check WATCHed the sessions-by-path id set and consulted `HGET le:{sid}:sess name` for each existing session — but `session_meta` lives on a different Cluster slot from the path index, so `HSET session_meta` could not join the MULTI/EXEC. Between EXEC and HSET, a concurrent `CreateSession` with the same name could observe the winner's `SADD` but read nil from its `HGET session_meta name` (HSET not yet run), pass its own duplicate check, and commit — two sessions with the same name. The fix introduces a same-slot composite uniqueness index `le:path:{hex(base_path)}:names` (members: `name\0session_id`) that shares the `{hex(base_path)}` hash tag with the legacy id set, so WATCH + SMEMBERS + SADD on both sets commit atomically in one slot-safe MULTI. `HSET session_meta` still runs post-EXEC but is no longer load-bearing for uniqueness. Orphan states (commit succeeded, HSET didn't) are invisible to `ListSessions` via the existing empty-metadata skip and opportunistically swept. (2) *`DeleteSession` cascade EXEC connection-drop mapped to `INTERNAL`.* `SessionStore::delete`'s MULTI/EXEC over `DEL session_meta` + `DEL layers_index` previously decoded the reply as `()`, which silently coerced both server-returned null and fred's `is_canceled()` into `Ok(())` — a mid-EXEC connection drop surfaced as `Internal` (gRPC `UNKNOWN`/`INTERNAL`) and clients did not retry, leaving half-cleaned state. The reply is now decoded as `fred::types::Value` with explicit null and `is_canceled` arms, both mapped to `Aborted("...retry_on_valkey_drop")` → gRPC `ABORTED` with distinct log-distinguishable messages. `DEL` is idempotent so a client retry either completes the cleanup or short-circuits with `NotFound` from the empty-HGETALL guard. This mirrors the contract `CreateSession` already carried. (3) *`DeleteSession` cascade amplification on Valkey outages.* The handler previously iterated every layer, aggregated `has_failures = true` on any non-NotFound error, and only returned `Aborted` at the end — so a 100-layer session hitting a flapping backend racked up 100 × (timeout × retry_max) work and reliably blew `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`. The handler now bails on the first non-NotFound error with an `Aborted` response carrying the progress counts (`layers_deleted_before_failure`, `layers_remaining`, `layers_total`), keeping one outage bounded to one per-layer budget. The idempotent-retry contract is unchanged — retries resume from the remaining layers via the NotFound-skip arm. **Client impact:** `CreateSession` callers competing on the same name and path now see at most one success even under high contention, and receive `AlreadyExists` reliably on the loser. `DeleteSession` callers during a Valkey blip now see `ABORTED` after a bounded per-layer attempt budget instead of `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` after unbounded amplification; retries succeed once Valkey recovers. No wire or API changes. Three related defense-in-depth items shipped alongside: `base_path` validation (length ≤ 4,096 B, no control chars, no null bytes — see Changed above); `acquire_session_cancel` now cleans up its `session_tokens` DashMap entry on any transient error via an RAII scope guard, not only on `NotFound`, closing a latent unbounded-map-growth surface; and `CreateSession`'s retry loop replaces the previous hardcoded 5-attempt no-jitter ceiling with a full-jitter backoff bounded by the new `LIVE_EDIT_SESSION_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default 32) and `LIVE_EDIT_SESSION_RETRY_INITIAL_DELAY_MS` (default 1). **Migration:** live-edit is pre-production and no migration is provided. Sessions created before this fix exist only in `path_index`, not `path_names_index`; they are invisible to the post-fix `ListSessions` and must be recreated via `CreateSession` to appear. No dev environment is expected to hit this.
- **OODF FFI hardened against invalid-UTF-8 input, cxx-bridge exception escape, and error-discriminant loss** (bug `b.8uk`). Three interlocking fixes on the OODF parse boundary. (1) *Invalid-UTF-8 in FlatBuffers strings no longer surfaces as `INTERNAL`*: the FlatBuffers verifier treats string fields as opaque byte vectors and does not validate UTF-8, so a verifier-clean buffer containing non-UTF-8 bytes inside any string field (`sourceFormat`, `sectionName`, `newName`, `keyName`) previously threw `std::invalid_argument` inside `rust::String`, was caught by the generic `catch (const std::exception& e)` at the FFI boundary, and mapped to `INTERNAL` with message `"unexpected C++ exception in parse_delta: ..."` — telling clients "server bug" when the correct answer was "your input was malformed." `oodf.cpp` now runs an explicit UTF-8 walk over every schema-declared string field before any `rust::String` is constructed and reports invalid UTF-8 as the new `OodfError::InvalidUtf8` variant, which maps to gRPC `INVALID_ARGUMENT` alongside `EmptyInput`, `TooShort`, `BadMagic`, `VerifierFailed`, and `UnknownCommandType`. (2) *Narrow `std::terminate` path closed*: the cxx bridge declarations (`fn parse_delta(...) -> ParseResult;`) do not produce an auto-generated try/catch shim, so a C++ exception escaping `parse_delta` would hit `std::terminate`. The explicit UTF-8 check in (1) moves the one realistic escape surface inside the existing in-function `try { ... } catch (...)` block, and every throwing code path now sits inside that boundary. (3) *Bridge-conversion errors are no longer stuffed inside `InternalError`*: when the bridge→idiomatic conversion in `oodf-ffi/src/convert.rs` encountered a `StructCommand` whose tag Rust did not map, the previous wrapper stringified the real error into `OodfError::InternalError(format!("bridge→idiomatic conversion failed despite Ok parse: {e}"))`, losing the typed discriminant and the command index. The wrapper now returns the original `OodfError` verbatim, so callers see `OodfError::UnknownCommandTagAt { index, tag }` with the exact position of the bad command — supporting better client retry / fallback behavior and better operator triage. **Breaking error-variant changes**: `OodfError::UnknownCommandTag(u8)` is renamed to `OodfError::UnknownCommandTagAt { index: usize, tag: u8 }` (added vector index); `OodfError::UnknownCommandType` payload narrowed from `u32` to `u8` to match the `[ubyte]` discriminant-vector type in `OmniObjectDelta.fbs`. Both variants are internal to `oodf-ffi` and surface to gRPC callers as `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; no wire-visible change. The service's `oodf-ffi` crate is not yet consumed by external clients outside this repo, so no downstream caller update is required.
- **Domain delta engine hardened against stack overflow, ghost section IDs, and unbounded per-delta input** (bug `b.55p`). Four domain-layer correctness bugs were bundled and fixed together because they all rely on the same `ObjectState` cross-map invariant. (1) *Stack-overflow DoS on deep section trees.* `DiffEngine::sections_in_dfs_order` — invoked on every `SubscribeReadLayer` catch-up — used a recursive DFS that blew the thread stack on a linear parent → child chain of ~10,000–80,000 sections, all trivially within the 100 MB layer cap. Any authenticated caller of `CreateLayer` / `UpdateLayer` could craft such a chain and crash replicas on subscribe. The walker is now iterative with an explicit stack and a hard depth cap of 10,000; deeper chains surface as `DiffError::SectionTreeTooDeep` → gRPC `INTERNAL` (the stored state, not the subscribing client, is malformed — so clients can safely re-subscribe against a healthy replica). (2) *Silent data loss via `CommandReorderChildren` ghost IDs.* The command overwrote `state.children[section_id]` verbatim with no validation that the listed IDs existed; the subsequent DFS would mark a ghost ID as "visited" and silently drop any later legitimate section that reused the same ID from the reconstructed diff. `CommandReorderChildren` now rejects with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (new `DeltaError::UnknownSectionInReorder`) if any listed ID is absent from the live section set. (3) *Duplicate-name slip past `CreateSection` / `MoveSection`.* `find_child_by_name` iterated only `state.children[parent_id]`, so an orphaned section (present in `state.sections` but absent from its parent's children list — a state the schema permits) with a colliding name was invisible to the duplicate-name check, and `CreateSection` admitted a second section with the same `(parent_id, name)`. The lookup now uses `state.sections` as the authoritative membership source; `state.children` governs ordering only. (4) *Unbounded per-delta input.* The OODF FlatBuffers verifier rejects malformed wire bytes and the 100 MB OMPF cap bounds total size, but nothing else limited per-primitive counts or per-name byte lengths — a single 100 MB layer could carry one 99 MB `key_name` or a `CommandReorderChildren` with millions of IDs. `apply_delta` now validates per-delta bounds up front: 100,000 entries each for `struct_commands` / `set_fields` / `time_samples` / `children_list`, and 4,096 bytes per name (`CreateSection.section_name`, `MoveSection.new_name`, `DiffChangeSection.section_name`, `SetField.key_name`). Breaches reject with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`DeltaError::DeltaBoundsExceeded { field, limit, actual }`) before any state mutation. Two additional defence-in-depth items shipped alongside: `state.sequence_num += 1` is now `checked_add` (a pinned `u64::MAX - 1` overflow is `INTERNAL` instead of silently wrapping to `0` and resetting every version-indexed filter), and `OmpfContainer::serialize` now returns `Result<Vec<u8>, OmpfError>` (rejects the architecturally-unreachable-but-not-typed-out-on-64-bit case `1 + external_values.len() > u32::MAX`). No wire or API changes; all new errors map to existing gRPC status codes.
- **Cross-replica presence and session-deletion fan-out now survive a Valkey reconnect** (bug `b.3z7`). On a Valkey failover or brief network blip, every replica's dedicated pub/sub connection previously dropped its `le:presence:events:{sid}` and `le:session:deleted` subscriptions silently — `PeerJoined` / `PeerLeft` broadcasts and cross-replica `DeleteSession` cancellations stopped firing on affected replicas until a pod restart, with no alert or auto-recovery. The service now spawns fred's `SubscriberClient::manage_subscriptions()` task at startup, which re-issues every tracked `SUBSCRIBE` after each reconnect event; the task is aborted before connections close during shutdown so it does not race the TCP teardown. Reconnect activity is observable via the new `live_edit_valkey_reconnects_total{pool="subscriber"}` counter.
- **Graceful shutdown is now bounded and fits inside Kubernetes `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`** (bug `b.3z7`). The previous sequential shutdown path (5-second HTTP/2 drain + four per-task joins at 5 seconds each + four sequential Valkey `quit()` calls at 2 seconds each ≈ 33 seconds worst-case) could exceed the default 30-second grace window, giving the kubelet a SIGKILL window that stranded in-flight cleanup work and sometimes left Valkey WATCH state on dropped connections. The service now (1) triggers all background-task cancellation tokens up front, (2) joins the six background tasks concurrently via `tokio::join!`, (3) runs all four Valkey `quit()` calls in parallel inside `ValkeyClient::quit`, and (4) wraps the entire post-server sequence in a single `tokio::time::timeout` sized by the new `LIVE_EDIT_SHUTDOWN_OVERALL_BUDGET_SECS` (default 30 s). The Helm chart's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` was raised 30 → 40 s to cover the HTTP/2 drain plus the bounded sequence with headroom. If the overall budget fires the process logs a warning and exits anyway rather than exceed the grace window. No client-facing wire change.
- **Prometheus `endpoint` label is now bounded against an attacker-controlled probe flood** (bug `b.3z7`). The gRPC metrics layer runs outside the auth interceptor, so an unauthenticated caller could previously reach it by probing arbitrary HTTP/2 paths — and each unique path became a new time series on `live_endpoint_calls_total` and `live_endpoint_duration_microseconds`, letting a trivial probe loop drive Prometheus to OOM by exploding cardinality. The `endpoint` label is now bucketed against a static allowlist of the ten real LiveService RPC method names (`CreateSession`, `DeleteSession`, `ListSessions`, `JoinSession`, `CreateLayer`, `UpdateLayer`, `DeleteLayer`, `StatLayer`, `SubscribeReadLayer`, `Ping`); anything else — including malformed paths — collapses to `endpoint="other"`. Operators adding a new RPC to the proto definition must add it to the allowlist in `src/grpc/metrics_layer.rs` in the same change, otherwise the new endpoint will appear under `"other"` in dashboards.
- **`init_tracing` is now idempotent** (bug `b.3z7`). A second call to `init_tracing` previously panicked under `tracing_subscriber::registry().init()`'s once-only contract, which is fine for normal production startup but hostile to tests and any startup-retry path. The function now logs a warning and no-ops on every subsequent call, mirroring the guard `build_otel_layer` picked up in b.q1d. No behavioral change on the happy path; no operator action required.
- **`DeleteSession` now reliably terminates `SubscribeReadLayer` and `JoinSession` streams across every replica, not just the one that handled the original `CreateSession`** (bug `b.uue`). On multi-replica deployments, a stream that load-balanced to a replica different from the `CreateSession` replica previously had no local cancellation token, so the cross-replica `DeleteSession` fanout (`le:session:deleted` pub/sub) had nothing to cancel and the stream kept running past the session's deletion. Subscribers and presence connections now terminate as documented in PRD §3.1.2 (`NOT_FOUND` for `SubscribeReadLayer`, graceful end for `JoinSession`) regardless of which replica they landed on. No client code change is required.
- **`UpdateLayer` now also retries through transient Valkey I/O blips, not just WATCH/MULTI/EXEC conflicts** (bug `b.1dz`). Follow-up to `b.vi2`. Transient fred errors (timeout, connection reset, cluster transition, command cancellation, backpressure) raised during the WATCH, HGETALL, snapshot-load, or MULTI-queue phases previously surfaced as gRPC `INTERNAL` on the first blip, even though a retry on a fresh connection would have succeeded — a pool connection mid-reconnect or a brief cluster reshard could fail the call. The server now classifies these as retriable and runs them through the same bounded retry loop as WATCH conflicts; `INTERNAL` is reserved for persistent failures (protocol / parse / config / auth / bad data). The exhaustion-path `ABORTED` message is now `"update_layer retriable failures, N retries exhausted (last cause: <underlying error>)"` (previously `"concurrent write conflict, ..."`). `LIVE_EDIT_UPDATE_LAYER_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` now caps retries for any of the three triggers — WATCH conflict, connection drop at EXEC, or transient Valkey I/O — rather than WATCH conflicts alone. **Client impact (`UpdateLayer`):** callers see fewer spurious `INTERNAL` errors during Valkey pod restarts or brief cluster state transitions; no client code change is required. **Client impact (`SubscribeReadLayer`):** the same classification also applies to the subscribe-path catch-up (`compute_diff` for out-of-window reconnects and the full-state load on brand-new subscribers), which has no server-side retry loop — a transient Valkey blip during subscriber catch-up therefore now surfaces as `ABORTED` instead of `INTERNAL`. This is strictly more-correct (clients can safely re-subscribe with the same `have_version` on `ABORTED`) but it is a user-visible status-code change on the subscribe path. No wire or API changes.
- **`UpdateLayer` no longer returns `ABORTED` on the first WATCH/MULTI/EXEC conflict; the server now retries transparently with full-jitter backoff** (bug `b.vi2`). Previously, any concurrent-writer collision on the same layer surfaced as gRPC `ABORTED` on the first attempt, and clients had to implement their own retry loops against stale state — the wire contract diverged from the established C++ live-edit service, which retries the same optimistic-concurrency pattern up to 512 times internally. The service now bounds the retry loop inside `UpdateLayer` itself: up to 32 attempts (configurable via the new `LIVE_EDIT_UPDATE_LAYER_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` env var) with full-jitter backoff (window capped at 50 ms) re-reading the header on every attempt, and `ABORTED` surfaces to the client only after the budget is exhausted. The exhaustion-path `ABORTED` message is now `"concurrent write conflict, N retries exhausted (last cause: <underlying error>)"` (N = the configured max) so clients and operators can distinguish retry-exhaustion from a single-shot conflict, and the `(last cause: ...)` suffix carries the final attempt's underlying error so operators can tell exhaustion after repeated nil-EXEC WATCH conflicts apart from exhaustion after repeated connection drops during EXEC. Under the 2026-04-22 dev-cluster perf harness at N=100 concurrent writers on one layer, ok% moved from 10.7 % → 100 % with the retry loop absorbing the WATCH conflicts. **Client impact:** clients MAY remove their own WATCH-conflict retry logic; `ABORTED` now means genuine load-shed rather than an ordinary concurrency outcome. Clients that still retry `ABORTED` will continue to work correctly (the retry is simply redundant against the now-rare exhaustion path). The only non-retriable failure modes on `UpdateLayer` — `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (`object_id` mismatch / header deleted), `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (malformed delta), `NOT_FOUND`, and `INTERNAL` — still surface immediately on the first attempt; the retry loop is narrowly scoped to `ABORTED`. No wire or API changes.
- **Presence and stream-cleanup resilience** (bug `b.k2t`). Three related resilience bugs fixed together. (1) *Zombie `JoinSession` connections after sweeper eviction.* The per-connection presence lease refresh loop ran `EXPIRE le:{sid}:presence:{cid}:lease 30`, which is a silent no-op if the key is already gone — so if the sweeper evicted the lease (or the key was never created due to a startup race), every subsequent tick kept spinning against a nonexistent key while the client still believed it was connected and while peer lists omitted the zombie. The refresh now uses `SET le:{sid}:presence:{cid}:lease 1 EX 30`, idempotently re-creating the lease on every tick and healing any evicted-key state. No operator action required; the fix takes effect on redeploy. (2) *Silent subscriber hang on `DeleteLayer` and `CreateLayer` overwrite.* The previous cleanup sequence `XADD deleted` → `UNLINK stream` raced any subscriber that happened to be between `XREAD BLOCK` round-trips when the UNLINK landed: the subscriber's next XREAD hit an empty/missing stream, never observed the `deleted` sentinel, and looped forever. The per-object stream key is now excluded from the UNLINK set and is instead `PEXPIRE`d with a 30-second grace window — Valkey's TTL reaps the key after the window while any lagging subscriber has time to wake up, read the sentinel, re-consult the layer mapping, and either re-bootstrap on the overwrite's new stream or exit `NOT_FOUND`. Applied symmetrically to the `DeleteLayer` path (SDD §7.11) and the `CreateLayer` overwrite path (SDD §7.9). No wire change; no operator action required.
- **External-value store crash-safety and GC correctness** (bug `b.i8x`). Two bugs in the external-value store could silently leak or permanently break content-hash entries on any non-graceful shutdown. (1) The write path used a four-phase `HSETNX` + `INCR` + `SET` + `HSET` dance; a crash between phases left a metadata hash pointing at `value_id=0` with no data ever written, and every subsequent reader got `DataNotFound` for that hash forever. (2) The garbage collector used `SPOP` to claim pending entries, then decremented and deleted; a crash between `SPOP` and the deletes leaked the meta + data rows because nothing ever revisited that hash. Both bugs are fixed by moving to a content-addressed schema with crash-safe ownership semantics: write path is now a single `MULTI { SET data; HINCRBY meta }` EXEC per hash (meta and data share a Cluster hash tag so EXEC is atomic); GC uses `SMOVE pending → inflight` to claim ownership, runs the decrement/delete, then `SREM inflight` to release, and startup drains any residual `inflight` entries back to `pending` so a crashed sweep is retried rather than leaked. **Key schema changed** — `le:ext:data:*` is now keyed by `{hash_hex}` (was `{value_id}`), `le:ext:meta:*` no longer carries a `value_id` field, the `le:counter:ext_value_id` counter is gone, and a new `le:ext:inflight` set is added. This is a pre-GA change with no production deployments; there is no migration shim. **Operators running pre-fix dev environments must flush their Valkey instance before redeploying** — otherwise the service will read stale keys under the old schema and behave unpredictably.
- **Orphan sweeper now covers every shard in a Valkey Cluster deployment.** The startup orphan sweep previously issued `SCAN` against a single cluster node, so on a multi-shard cluster it silently missed orphaned object keys and orphaned layer mappings on every shard except the one `fred`'s connection pool happened to pick. Orphaned state therefore accumulated unboundedly on all other shards with no error and no metric signal. The sweeper now detects clustered deployments and routes both scan loops through `scan_cluster_buffered` (enumerating all shards); non-clustered deployments continue to use single-node `scan_buffered` unchanged. **Operators running multi-shard clusters should redeploy to pick up this fix** — after redeploy, the first startup sweep on each replica will clean up the backlog of orphans that accumulated under the pre-fix behavior.
- Wire-format incompatibility with the legacy C++ live-edit service. The service now produces and consumes OODF FlatBuffers bytes that are byte-compatible with existing C++ clients, fixing a schema-level mismatch in the `structCommands` field that previously required a non-conformant workaround.
- **Silent dropped writes under concurrent `UpdateLayer`.** When two or more clients raced on the same layer, writers that lost the WATCH/MULTI/EXEC race previously returned `Ok` with a `sequence_num` to the caller even though no commands committed server-side — a single `XADD` landed on the per-object stream while every racer reported success. Concurrent losers now correctly surface the transaction conflict as gRPC `ABORTED` with the message `"concurrent write conflict, retry"`, matching the optimistic-concurrency contract in SDD §7.5 / §18.2. Clients MUST retry on `ABORTED` per PRD §4.2. `CreateSession` had the same latent bug on its path-index SADD transaction and is fixed at the same time (bee `b.8q1`).

### Performance
- **`UpdateLayer` field / time-sample writes now commit in a single Valkey round-trip via atomic server-side Lua EVAL, closing the Rust-vs-C++ throughput gap at high single-object concurrency** (bug `b.67u`). The common-case delta shape (field and time-sample updates with no structural commands — the overwhelming majority of updates under sustained collaborative editing) previously took 2–3 round-trips per attempt against the exclusive transaction pool (WATCH + HGETALL, MULTI, EXEC) and spun through the server-side retry loop on every WATCH conflict. The server now dispatches these deltas through an atomic Lua script instead — no WATCH, no MULTI/EXEC, no client-side retry on conflict (Lua runs atomically on Valkey's single thread). Structural-command deltas continue to use the legacy WATCH/MULTI/EXEC path. Clients see no wire-contract change — the gRPC surface, stream payload, and error mapping are identical. Expected throughput impact at N concurrent writers on one layer: marginal at N=10, ~40–80 % at N=50, ~50–100 % at N=100. Operators who need to roll back can set the new `LIVE_EDIT_UPDATE_LAYER_BACKEND=txn` env var (default `lua`), which forces every `UpdateLayer` call down the legacy path; `txn`-mode output is byte-for-byte identical to the pre-b.67u service.
- **`UpdateLayer` concurrent-write throughput improved 3.5–4.5× on single-layer contention** (bug `b.mnu`). Field-only / time-sample-only deltas (the common write shape) now skip the full object-state snapshot load inside the WATCH/MULTI/EXEC window, collapsing each attempt from ~20 ms to ~3–5 ms on dev-cluster RTTs. Under the new server-side retry loop (b.vi2), this translates to fewer retries per commit and lower Valkey CPU per successful `UpdateLayer`. No wire or API changes.

## Initial implementation - 2026-04-16

Written 2026-04-16, the day before the first release candidate (`0.1.0-rc.1`, 2026-04-17), and kept as written.
These changes first shipped in 0.1.0.

### Added
- Initial `nvidia.omniverse.live.v1alpha.LiveService` gRPC API for real-time collaborative editing of Omniverse scene layers.
- Session management RPCs: `CreateSession`, `DeleteSession`, and `ListSessions` for creating, removing, and discovering editing sessions.
- Layer operations: `CreateLayer` and `UpdateLayer` accept client-streaming chunked OMPF uploads for initial layer content and FlatBuffers deltas respectively. `DeleteLayer` and `StatLayer` provide layer lifecycle management and metadata queries.
- Real-time subscription via `SubscribeReadLayer` server-streaming RPC delivers catch-up diffs from a given sequence number followed by live delta notifications. Self-echo suppression filters out a subscriber's own writes.
- Presence and peer messaging via `JoinSession` bidirectional-streaming RPC with peer join/leave notifications and arbitrary data relay between connected clients.
- `Ping` unary RPC for round-trip latency diagnostics with client and server timestamps.
- Bearer token authentication forwarded to external permission and userinfo services for path-level authorization and user identity resolution.
